<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When creating pull requests, create only ONE per session/issue unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* When working with an existing PR, update it with new commits rather than creating additional PRs for the same issue.
* When updating a PR, preserve the original PR title and purpose, updating description only when necessary.
</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROBLEM_SOLVING_WORKFLOW>

3. TESTING:

</ENVIRONMENT_SETUP>

  3. Methodically address the most likely causes, starting with the highest probability

  - Confirm whether they want it as a separate file or just in the conversation

</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

     1	# Publ
     2	
     3	A personal publishing platform. Like a static publishing system, only dynamic.
     4	
     5	## Motivation
     6	
     7	I make a lot of different things — comics, music, art, code, games — and none of
     8	the existing content management systems I found quite satisfy my use cases.
     9	Either they don't allow enough flexibility in the sorts of content that they can
    10	provide, or the complexity in managing the content makes it more complicated than
    11	simply hand-authoring a site.
    12	
    13	I want to bring the best of the classic static web to a more dynamic publishing
    14	system; scheduled posts, category-based templates, and built-in support for
    15	image renditions (including thumbnails, high-DPI support, and image galleries).
    16	And I want to do it all using simple Markdown files organized in a sensible
    17	file hierarchy.
    18	
    19	## Basic tenets
    20	
    21	* Containerized web app that's deployable with little friction (hopefully)
    22	* Do one thing (present heterogeneous content), do it well (hopefully)
    23	* Use external tools for site content editing
    24	* Be CDN-friendly
    25	* High-DPI images and image sets as first-class citizens
    26	* Interoperate with everything that's open for interoperation (especially [IndieWeb](http://indieweb.org))
    27	
    28	## See it in action
    29	
    30	The main demonstration site is at http://beesbuzz.biz/ — it is of course a
    31	work in progress! The documentation site for Publ itself (which is also a work in progress) lives at http://publ.beesbuzz.biz/
    32	
    33	## Operating requirements
    34	
    35	I am designing this to work in any WSGI-capable environment with Python 3.5+. This
    36	means that it will, for example, be deployable on any shared hosting which
    37	has Passenger support (such as Dreamhost), as well as on Heroku, Google AppEngine,
    38	S3, or any other simple containerized deployment target.
    39	
    40	The file system is the ground truth for all site data, and while it does use a
    41	database as a content index, the actual choice of database shouldn't matter all
    42	that much. I am targeting SQLite for development, but mysql and Postgres should
    43	be supported as well.
    44	
    45	## Additional resources
    46	
    47	The [Publ-site](https://github.com/PlaidWeb/Publ-site) repository stores all of the templates, site content, and configuration for the [Publ site](http://publ.beesbuzz.biz).
    48	
    49	The [Publ-templates-beesbuzz.biz](https://github.com/PlaidWeb/Publ-templates-beesbuzz.biz) repository provides a stripped-down sample site based on [my personal homepage](http://beesbuzz.biz).
    50	
    51	## Authors
    52	
    53	In order of first contribution:
    54	
    55	* [fluffy](https://github.com/fluffy-critter)
    56	* [karinassuni](https://github.com/karinassuni)
    57

     4	# Run with a parameter of 'fast' to skip the package install.

     1	""" tests of publ.utils class """
     2	
     3	from publ.utils import TagSet
     4	
     5	
     6	def test_tagset_membership():
     7	    """ Membership tests for TagSet """
     8	    items = ('a', 'S', 'd', 'F')
     9	    others = ('Q', 'w', 'E', 'r')
    10	    tags = TagSet(items)
    11	
    12	    assert bool(tags)
    13	    assert not bool(TagSet())
    14	    assert not bool(TagSet([]))
    15	    assert not bool(TagSet({}))
    16	
    17	    assert len(tags) == 4
    18	
    19	    assert hash(TagSet(('a', 's', 'd'))) == hash(TagSet(('s', 'd', 'a')))
    20	    assert hash(TagSet(('a', 's', 'd'))) == hash(TagSet(('A', 'S', 'D')))
    21	    assert hash(TagSet(items)) != hash(TagSet(others))
    22	
    23	    for item in items:
    24	        assert item in tags
    25	        assert item.lower() in tags
    26	        assert item.upper() in tags
    27	        assert item.casefold() in tags
    28	
    29	    for item in tags:
    30	        assert item.casefold() in {t.casefold() for t in items}
    31	
    32	    for item in others:
    33	        assert item not in tags
    34	        assert item.lower() not in tags
    35	        assert item.upper() not in tags
    36	        assert item.casefold() not in tags
    37	
    38	
    39	def test_tagset_operators():
    40	    """ Test operators  on TagSet """
    41	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'D', 'F')) == TagSet(('A', 'S', 'd', 'f'))
    42	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'D', 'F')) == {'A', 'S', 'd', 'f'}
    43	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f')) != TagSet(('a', 's', 'd'))
    44	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f')) != {'a', 's', 'd'}
    45	
    46	    assert TagSet(('a', 'S', 'd')) | TagSet(('A', 's', 'D', 'F')) == TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f'))
    47	    assert TagSet(('a', 'S', 'd')) | {'A', 's', 'D', 'F'} == TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f'))
    48	
    49	    assert TagSet(('a', 'S', 'd')) & TagSet(('a', 'D')) == TagSet(('A', 'd'))
    50	    assert TagSet(('a', 'S', 'd')) & {'a', 'D'} == TagSet(('A', 'd'))
    51	
    52	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f')) ^ TagSet(
    53	        ('A', 'F', 'G', 'h')) == TagSet(('s', 'd', 'g', 'h'))
    54	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f')) ^ {'A', 'F', 'G', 'h'} == TagSet(('s', 'd', 'g', 'h'))
    55	
    56	    assert TagSet(('a', 's', 'd', 'f')) - {'A', 'D', 'g', 'G'} == {'s', 'f'}
    57	
    58	    assert TagSet(('1', '2', '3')) < TagSet(('1', '2', '3', '4'))
    59	    assert TagSet(('1', '2', '3')) < {'1', '2', '3', '4'}
    60	
    61	    assert TagSet(('1', '2', '3')) <= TagSet(('1', '2', '3', '4'))
    62	    assert TagSet(('1', '2', '3')) <= {'1', '2', '3', '4'}
    63	
    64	    # pylint:disable=unneeded-not
    65	    assert not TagSet(('a', 's', 'd')) < TagSet(('a', 's', 'd'))
    66	    assert not TagSet(('a', 's', 'd')) < TagSet(('q', 'w', 'e'))
    67	    assert not TagSet(('a', 's', 'd')) <= TagSet(('q', 'w', 'e'))
    68

     2	
     3	from distutils.util import convert_path
     4	from os import path
     5	
     6	# Always prefer setuptools over distutils
     7	from setuptools import find_packages, setup
     8	
     9	here = path.abspath(path.dirname(__file__))
    10	
    11	# Get the long description from the README file
    12	with open(path.join(here, 'README.md')) as f:
    13	    long_description = f.read()
    14	
    15	main_ns = {}
    16	ver_path = convert_path('publ/__version__.py')
    17	with open(ver_path) as ver_file:
    18	    exec(ver_file.read(), main_ns)

    20

    28	    long_description=long_description,

    31	        """ Constructor for a Publ application. Accepts the following parameters:
    32	
    33	        name -- The name of the app
    34	        cfg -- Application configuration
    35	
    36	        Additional keyword arguments are forwarded along to the Flask constructor.
    37	
    38	        Configuration keys:
    39	
    40	        database_config -- The database confugiration to be provided to PonyORM.
    41	            See https://docs.ponyorm.org/database.html for more information
    42	        content_folder -- The folder that stores the site content
    43	        template_folder -- The folder that contains the Jinja templates

    47	            store the image rendition cache

    50	        image_cache_interval -- How frequently (in seconds) to clean up the

    54	        cache -- Page render cache configuration; see
    55	            https://flask-caching.readthedocs.io/en/latest/#configuring-flask-caching
    56	        markdown_extensions -- The extensions to enable by default for the

    58	            for details

    60	            The default value is randomly generated at every application restart.
    61	        auth -- Authentication configuration. See the Authl configuration
    62	            documentation at [link TBD]. Additionally, setting the key
    63	            AUTH_FORCE_HTTPS to a truthy value can be used to force the user to
    64	            switch to an SSL connection when they log in.
    65	        user_list -- The file that configures the user and group list

   212	                                                   config.auth_log_prune_age),
   213	                                 config.auth_log_prune_interval)

   216

   218	            self.after_request(self._set_cache_expiry)
   219	
   220	        if self.debug:
   221	            # We're in debug mode so we don't want to scan until everything's up
   222	            # and running
   223	            self.before_first_request(self._startup)
   224	        else:
   225	            # In production, register the exception handler and scan the index
   226	            # immediately
   227	            self.register_error_handler(Exception, rendering.render_exception)
   228	            self._startup()
   229	
   230	    def path_alias_regex(self, regex):
   231	        r""" A decorator that adds a path-alias regular expression; calls
   232	        add_path_regex.
   233	
   234	        Example usage:
   235	
   236	        @app.path_alias_regex(r'/d/([0-9]{8}(_w)?)\.php')
   237	        def redirect_date(match):
   238	            return flask.url_for('category', category='comics',
   239	                                 date=match.group(1)), True
   240	        """
   241	        def decorator(func):
   242	            """ Adds the function to the regular expression alias list """
   243	            return self.add_path_regex(regex, func)
   244	        return decorator
   245	
   246	    def add_path_regex(self, regex, func):
   247	        """ Add a path-alias regex callback to the request router. Takes the
   248	        following arguments:
   249	
   250	        regex -- The regular expression for the path-alias hook
   251	        f -- A function taking a `re.match` object on successful match, and
   252	            returns a tuple of `(url, is_permanent)`; url can be `None` if the
   253	            function decides it should not redirect after all.
   254	
   255	        The function may also use `flask.request.args` or the like if it needs
   256	        to make a determination based on query args.
   257	        """
   258	        self._regex_map.append((regex, func))
   259	        return func
   260	
   261	    def test_path_regex(self, path):
   262	        """ Evaluate the registered path-alias regular expressions. Returns the
   263	        result of the first handler that successfully matches the path.
   264	        """
   265	        for regex, func in self._regex_map:
   266	            match = re.match(regex, path)
   267	            if match:
   268	                dest, permanent = func(match)
   269	                if dest:
   270	                    return dest, permanent
   271	
   272	        return None, None
   273	
   274	    @staticmethod
   275	    def _startup():
   276	        """ Startup routine for initiating the content indexer """
   277	        model.setup()
   278	        index.scan_index(config.content_folder)
   279	        index.background_scan(config.content_folder)
   280	
   281	    @staticmethod
   282	    def _set_cache_expiry(response):
   283	        """ Set the cache control headers """
   284	        if response.cache_control.max_age is None and 'CACHE_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT' in config.cache:
   285	            response.cache_control.max_age = config.cache['CACHE_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT']
   286	        return response
   287

   291	    LOGGER.warning("This function is deprecated; use publ.Publ instead")
   292	    return Publ(name, cfg)

""" Integration tests for Publ """

import os
import re
from unittest.mock import patch

def setup_test_environment():
    """Set up the test environment"""
    # Set up the environment similar to how app.py does it
    os.environ['TEST_CACHING'] = 'simple'
    
    # Change to tests directory
    old_cwd = os.getcwd()
    test_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    os.chdir(test_dir)
    
    return old_cwd

def teardown_test_environment(old_cwd):
    """Restore the original environment"""
    os.chdir(old_cwd)

def test_homepage():
    """Test that the homepage loads correctly"""
    old_cwd = setup_test_environment()
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        
        # Create a test client
        client = app.app.test_client()
        
        # Test basic route
        response = client.get('/')
        assert response.status_code == 200
        assert b'<title>' in response.data
        assert b'Publ' in response.data
        
        print("✓ Homepage test passed!")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Homepage test failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        teardown_test_environment(old_cwd)

def test_simple_entry():
    """Test that a simple entry loads correctly"""
    old_cwd = setup_test_environment()
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        
        # Create a test client
        client = app.app.test_client()
        
        # Test the simple test entry (based on the content file)
        # We need to find the entry by scanning or by known ID
        response = client.get('/308')  # Entry-ID from simple test entry.md
        if response.status_code == 200:
            assert b'This is a simple test entry' in response.data
            assert b'incredibly basic test entry' in response.data
            print("✓ Simple entry test passed!")
            return True
        elif response.status_code == 404:
            # Try alternative URL patterns
            response = client.get('/308-')
            if response.status_code == 200:
                assert b'This is a simple test entry' in response.data
                print("✓ Simple entry test passed (with dash)!")
                return True
            
            # Try with slug
            response = client.get('/308-simple-test-entry')
            if response.status_code == 200:
                assert b'This is a simple test entry' in response.data
                print("✓ Simple entry test passed (with slug)!")
                return True
                
        print(f"✗ Simple entry test failed: status {response.status_code}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Simple entry test failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        teardown_test_environment(old_cwd)

def test_multiple_routes():
    """Test multiple common routes to ensure they load"""
    old_cwd = setup_test_environment()
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        
        # Create a test client
        client = app.app.test_client()
        
        # Test various routes that should exist
        routes_to_test = [
            '/',
            '/tags/',
            '/categories/',
            '/auth/',
            '/images/',
            '/cards/',
            '/footnotes/',
            '/entity-titles/',
            '/markdown-titles/',
            '/subcats/',
        ]
        
        passed = 0
        total = len(routes_to_test)
        
        for route in routes_to_test:
            try:
                response = client.get(route)
                if response.status_code == 200:
                    passed += 1
                    print(f"✓ Route {route} loaded successfully")
                else:
                    print(f"⚠ Route {route} returned status {response.status_code}")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"✗ Route {route} failed: {e}")
        
        print(f"✓ Multiple routes test: {passed}/{total} routes passed")
        return passed > 0  # At least some should pass
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Multiple routes test failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        teardown_test_environment(old_cwd)

def test_page_capture_and_comparison():
    """Test that we can capture page output for comparison"""
    old_cwd = setup_test_environment()
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        
        # Create a test client
        client = app.app.test_client()
        
        # Get homepage content
        response = client.get('/')
        if response.status_code == 200:
            content = response.get_data(as_text=True)
            
            # Basic checks that content is reasonable
            assert '<html' in content.lower()
            assert '<title>' in content.lower()
            assert len(content) > 100  # Should have substantial content
            
            # Store for potential reference comparison
            print(f"✓ Page capture test passed! Captured {len(content)} characters")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Page capture test failed: status {response.status_code}")
            return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Page capture test failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        teardown_test_environment(old_cwd)

def test_scenario_testing():
    """Test scenario-based testing capabilities"""
    old_cwd = setup_test_environment()
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        
        # Create a test client
        client = app.app.test_client()
        
        # Test different scenarios:
        # 1. Regular page access
        response = client.get('/')
        assert response.status_code == 200
        
        # 2. Non-existent page
        response = client.get('/nonexistent-page')
        # Either 404 or redirect is acceptable
        assert response.status_code in [404, 301, 302]
        
        # 3. Test with different HTTP methods where appropriate
        response = client.post('/', data={})
        # Should handle POST gracefully
        assert response.status_code in [200, 405]  # OK or Method Not Allowed
        
        print("✓ Scenario testing capabilities verified!")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"✗ Scenario testing failed: {e}")
        return False
    finally:
        teardown_test_environment(old_cwd)

def run_all_tests():
    """Run all integration tests"""
    print("Running Publ integration tests...")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    tests = [
        test_homepage,
        test_simple_entry,
        test_multiple_routes,
        test_page_capture_and_comparison,
        test_scenario_testing,
    ]
    
    passed = 0
    total = len(tests)
    
    for test in tests:
        if test():
            passed += 1
        print()  # Add spacing between tests
    
    print("=" * 50)
    print(f"Integration tests completed: {passed}/{total} tests passed")
    
    if passed == total:
        print("🎉 All integration tests passed!")
        return True
    else:
        print(f"⚠️  {total - passed} tests failed")
        return False

if __name__ == '__main__':
    success = run_all_tests()
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

Let me also create a test runner script that can be used as mentioned in the issue:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PlaidWeb__Publ__0.5/tests/test_runner.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
""" Test runner for Publ integration tests """

import argparse
import sys
import os

def run_integration_tests(capture_output=False, compare_reference=False):
    """
    Run integration tests for Publ
    
    Args:
        capture_output: Whether to capture and store page outputs
        compare_reference: Whether to compare against reference outputs
    
    Returns:
        bool: True if all tests pass, False otherwise
    """
    print("Publ Integration Test Runner")
    print("=" * 40)
    
    # Import and run integration tests
    try:
        import test_integration
        return test_integration.run_all_tests()
    except ImportError as e:
        print(f"Failed to import test_integration: {e}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error running integration tests: {e}")
        return False

def run_unit_tests():
    """Run unit tests"""
    print("Running unit tests...")
    try:
        import subprocess
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "test_utils.py", "-v"], 
                              cwd=os.path.dirname(__file__), capture_output=True, text=True)
        print(result.stdout)
        if result.stderr:
            print(result.stderr)
        return result.returncode == 0
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error running unit tests: {e}")
        return False

def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Publ Test Runner')
    parser.add_argument('--integration', action='store_true', 
                       help='Run integration tests')
    parser.add_argument('--unit', action='store_true',
                       help='Run unit tests')
    parser.add_argument('--all', action='store_true',
                       help='Run all tests')
    parser.add_argument('--capture', action='store_true',
                       help='Capture page outputs for comparison')
    parser.add_argument('--compare', action='store_true',
                       help='Compare outputs with reference')
    
    args = parser.parse_args()
    
    # If no specific test type requested, run all
    if not (args.integration or args.unit or args.all):
        args.all = True
    
    success = True
    
    if args.all or args.unit:
        print("Running unit tests...")
        if not run_unit_tests():
            success = False
    
    if args.all or args.integration:
        print("\nRunning integration tests...")
        if not run_integration_tests(args.capture, args.compare):
            success = False
    
    return 0 if success else 1

if __name__ == '__main__':
    sys.exit(main())</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PlaidWeb__Publ__0.5/tests/test_runner.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PlaidWeb__Publ__0.5/tests && chmod +x test_runner.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>

""" Pytest integration tests for Publ """

import os
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def test_client():
    """Create a test client fixture"""
    # Set up the environment
    original_cwd = os.getcwd()
    test_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    os.chdir(test_dir)
    os.environ['TEST_CACHING'] = 'simple'
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        client = app.app.test_client()
        yield client
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)


def test_homepage_loads(test_client):
    """Test that the homepage loads correctly"""
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert b'<title>' in response.data
    assert b'Publ' in response.data


def test_category_pages_load(test_client):
    """Test that various category pages load"""
    categories = ['/', '/tags/', '/categories/', '/auth/', '/images/']
    
    for category in categories:
        response = test_client.get(category)
        assert response.status_code == 200, f"Failed to load {category}"


def test_error_handling(test_client):
    """Test error handling for non-existent pages"""
    response = test_client.get('/nonexistent-page')
    # Should either return 404 or redirect appropriately
    assert response.status_code in [404, 301, 302]


def test_http_methods(test_client):
    """Test handling of different HTTP methods"""
    # GET should work
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    # POST should be handled gracefully
    response = test_client.post('/', data={})
    assert response.status_code in [200, 405]  # OK or Method Not Allowed


def test_page_content_capture(test_client):
    """Test that we can capture page content"""
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    content = response.get_data(as_text=True)
    assert '<html' in content.lower()
    assert '<title>' in content.lower()
    assert len(content) > 100  # Should have substantial content


def test_scenario_testing(test_client):
    """Test various scenario testing capabilities"""
    # Test regular page access
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    # Test non-existent page handling
    response = test_client.get('/definitely-does-not-exist')
    assert response.status_code in [404, 301, 302]
    
    # Test POST handling
    response = test_client.post('/nonexistent-form', data={'test': 'data'})

""" Pytest integration tests for Publ """

import os
import sys
import pytest


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def test_client():
    """Create a test client fixture"""
    # Set up the environment
    original_cwd = os.getcwd()
    test_dir = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    os.chdir(test_dir)
    os.environ['TEST_CACHING'] = 'simple'
    
    # Add tests directory to Python path
    sys.path.insert(0, test_dir)
    
    try:
        # Import the app after setting environment
        import app
        client = app.app.test_client()
        yield client
    finally:
        os.chdir(original_cwd)
        if test_dir in sys.path:
            sys.path.remove(test_dir)


def test_homepage_loads(test_client):
    """Test that the homepage loads correctly"""
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert b'<title>' in response.data
    assert b'Publ' in response.data


def test_category_pages_load(test_client):
    """Test that various category pages load"""
    categories = ['/', '/tags/', '/categories/', '/auth/', '/images/']
    
    for category in categories:
        response = test_client.get(category)
        assert response.status_code == 200, f"Failed to load {category}"


def test_error_handling(test_client):
    """Test error handling for non-existent pages"""
    response = test_client.get('/nonexistent-page')
    # Should return an error status code (4xx or 5xx)
    assert response.status_code >= 400


def test_http_methods(test_client):
    """Test handling of different HTTP methods"""
    # GET should work
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    # POST should be handled gracefully
    response = test_client.post('/', data={})
    assert response.status_code in [200, 405]  # OK or Method Not Allowed


def test_page_content_capture(test_client):
    """Test that we can capture page content"""
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    content = response.get_data(as_text=True)
    assert '<html' in content.lower()
    assert '<title>' in content.lower()
    assert len(content) > 100  # Should have substantial content


def test_scenario_testing(test_client):
    """Test various scenario testing capabilities"""
    # Test regular page access
    response = test_client.get('/')
    assert response.status_code == 200
    
    # Test non-existent page handling
    response = test_client.get('/definitely-does-not-exist')
    assert response.status_code >= 400
    
    # Test POST handling
    response = test_client.post('/nonexistent-form', data={'test': 'data'})
